========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate you taking the time. I hope I'm in the right place for this... 

I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble lately, mostly with work. I'm an order filler at a warehouse, and they recently changed my hours and brought in this whole new inventory system. It's... well, I should be able to handle it, I know that, but I feel like I'm just sort of falling apart over it. 

It's a little embarrassing to admit, but I've actually missed a few shifts, and even skipped out on some of my church commitments, which really isn't like me at all. I've been snapping at my family a bit, too, and I feel terrible about it. Some mornings I just sit on the edge of the bed in my work boots, just staring, wondering if I can even face the place anymore. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this right... is that the kind of thing you can help with?

Going through the motions? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I definitely still have a purpose. I mean, I go to work at the warehouse every day, and I make sure I do a good job filling the orders. They rely on me there, you know? I wouldn't want to let them down by just... phoning it in.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Weight? Oh, no, I don't think so. My clothes all seem to fit about the same as they always have. I haven't noticed any big changes or anything like that. 

I mean, I suppose I haven't really been weighing myself, but I'm still eating. I make sure I have my meals. Maybe my appetite isn't quite what it was when my wife was cooking, but I'm definitely not starving myself or wasting away. I know it's important to keep my strength up for the warehouse, so I make a point to eat. 

I'm taking good care of myself, I really am. That's what you're supposed to do, right? Just keep going and take care of your health?

No, I don't think so. I really don't. I mean, I know we just talked about this, but I'm still finding plenty of things to enjoy. I still like tending to the yard a bit on my days off, and I still watch the evening news.

Well, besides losing my wife, which we talked about... I suppose there has been one other thing. I know it probably sounds silly to get worked up over a job at my age, but they recently changed my hours at the warehouse. And they brought in this whole new inventory system. I'm trying my absolute best to keep up with it, I really am. 

But... well, to be perfectly honest, ever since they made those changes, I kind of feel like I'm falling apart a little bit. I hate to even admit it, because I don't want to sound like a complainer, but I've actually missed a couple of shifts. And I haven't been making it to my church commitments, which I feel just terrible about. I've even been snapping at my family, and they don't deserve that. Sometimes... sometimes in the mornings, I just sit there on the edge of the bed in my work boots, and I just wonder if I can even face going into the place anymore. 

It's just a lot to learn all at once, you know? That's a normal way to react to a big schedule change, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or anything, because I really am trying to handle it the right way.

I just don't want you thinking I've turned into some grumpy old man who can't find joy in anything anymore. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how I'm handling everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Oh, no, my appetite is just fine. I'm still eating my regular meals, breakfast, lunch, and dinner. I don't want you to think I'm wasting away or not taking care of myself, because I really am. \n\nMy wife always made sure we had good, hearty meals, and I've tried to keep up with that, even if it's just me cooking now. I'm definitely not skipping meals or anything like that. I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially with the job. \n\nI hope that's what you were looking for? I just want to reassure you that I'm staying healthy and eating right. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Not real guilt. I certainly haven't done anything wrong that I'm dwelling on. I try to keep a clean conscience. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these right for you. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they feel like a chore. I still like doing the things I've always done. I mean, I still keep my yard looking nice, and I read the paper every morning. I'm not just sitting around the house all day staring at the wall, I promise. \n\nI guess... well, I haven't been out fishing in a few weeks, but that's just because the weather hasn't been great, and I've been a little tired from work. I just don't want you to worry that I'm giving up on things or losing interest in life, because I'm really not. I'm still keeping busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're going through the motions of your daily routine without any real motivation or purpose?\n\nPatient: Going through the motions? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I definitely still have a purpose. I mean, I go to work at the warehouse every day, and I make sure I do a good job filling the orders. They rely on me there, you know? I wouldn't want to let them down by just... phoning it in. \n\nI still have my routine, and I stick to it because it's important to stay productive. I get up, I make my coffee, I head to work... I'm not just wandering aimlessly, I promise you. I know it's just a stocking job, but it gives me a reason to get out of the house and keep moving. \n\nI guess maybe some days are a little harder to get started than others, especially since my wife passed, but I always push through. I'm not giving up on my responsibilities. \n\nDoes that answer your question alright? I just want to make sure you know I'm still trying my best every single day. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in activities that normally bring you pleasure over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Less interested? Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still enjoy my routine, you know? I still like getting my coffee in the morning and sitting on the porch. I haven't lost interest in the things that matter.\n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as eager to putter around in the garage lately, but that's just because I've been a bit tired after my shifts at the warehouse. It's not that I don't find pleasure in it anymore. I just need a little more rest these days, being 74 and all. I'm still the same old me, I promise.\n\nI hope I'm making sense. I just don't want you to think I'm sitting around feeling sorry for myself or losing my spark, because I'm really not. I'm doing alright, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not getting as much pleasure out of things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I still get plenty of enjoyment out of things. I mean, my morning coffee still tastes just as good, and I still like reading the paper. I'm not... I'm not finding everything dull or anything like that. \n\nI guess maybe... well, maybe things just feel a little quieter lately, you know? Since my wife's been gone, the house is just a bit still. I don't want you to think I'm just miserable all the time, because I'm really trying to keep a good attitude. \n\nI'm doing the right things, aren't I? I just want to make sure I'm handling this all the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Oh, no, I don't think so. I haven't noticed any big changes or anything like that. \n\nI mean, I suppose I haven't really been weighing myself, but I'm still eating. I make sure I have my meals. I just prefer to make my toast here at home now. It's perfectly fine. \n\nI'm still doing okay, aren't I? I mean, it's normal to change your routine a little bit at my age, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life changes or losses recently, such as the death of a loved one or a move to a new home?\n\nPatient: Well, besides losing my wife, which we talked about... And they brought in this whole new inventory system. I'm trying my absolute best to keep up with it, I really am. \n\nBut... well, to be perfectly honest, ever since they made those changes, I kind of feel like I'm falling apart a little bit. I hate to even admit it, because I don't want to sound like a complainer, but I've actually missed a couple of shifts. And I haven't been making it to my church commitments, which I feel just terrible about. I've even been snapping at my family, and they don't deserve that. Sometimes... sometimes in the mornings, I just sit there on the edge of the bed in my work boots, and I just wonder if I can even face going into the place anymore. \n\nIt's just a lot to learn all at once, you know? I really don't want you to think I'm just making up excuses to avoid hard work. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around feeling sorry for myself, or crying or anything like that. I've got plenty to be grateful for, you know? \n\nI'm not depressed, I promise. It's really just... well, like I said, just feeling sort of stuck when I try to get out the door. But not down in the dumps. I know guys my age shouldn't complain, and I really try to keep a positive attitude about things. \n\nI hope I'm not making this too confusing for you... am I explaining things alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um, lost interest? I wouldn't say that exactly. I mean, I still like the things I've always liked. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up on life or anything. \n\nI did mention I missed a few church things lately, but that's not because I don't care about them anymore. I really do. It's just been a little harder to get myself going with all this work stuff hanging over my head. Oh, well, no, I wouldn't say I'm unusually tired. I mean, I'm 74, so I guess I'm not exactly a spring chicken, but I've got plenty of energy. I really don't want you to think I'm just getting too old or physically worn out for the warehouse. I've always been a hard worker. \n\nIt's not a lack of energy, I promise. I don't want to come across like I'm just feeling lazy or lethargic, because that's really not it at all. My physical energy is just fine. \n\nDoes that help clear things up? I just want to make sure I'm not giving you the wrong impression about my health. Am I answering these the right way for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you still get pleasure from the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. I still get a lot of enjoyment out of things. I really do. I mean, I don't want to sound like a broken record, but it's just like I said before... I still like my life. \n\nWhen I actually get to church, or when I'm spending time with my grandkids, it's wonderful. I smile, I laugh... I'm not sitting there miserable, I promise. It's just the getting there that's been a little tricky lately with everything on my mind. But the actual enjoyment? That's still there, one hundred percent. \n\nI hope I'm making sense? I just don't want you thinking I've turned into some grumpy old man who can't find joy in anything anymore. That's not me at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain tasks or situations because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: Avoiding tasks? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I still show up for all my shifts at the warehouse, and I make sure to get my chores done around the house. I really don't want you to think I'm the kind of person who just hides from his responsibilities when things get a little tough. \n\nI've always tried to face things head-on, you know? I know I mentioned earlier that I missed a couple of church services, but that wasn't because I was hiding or... or overwhelmed by the situation. I promise I'm not just slacking off or running away from things. \n\nIs that... does that answer what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how I'm handling everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Oh, no, my appetite is just fine. I'm still eating my regular meals, breakfast, lunch, and dinner. I don't want you to think I'm wasting away or not taking care of myself, because I really am. \n\nMy wife always made sure we had good, hearty meals, and I've tried to keep up with that, even if it's just me cooking now. I'm definitely not skipping meals or anything like that. I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially with the job. \n\nI hope that's what you were looking for? I just want to reassure you that I'm staying healthy and eating right. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't think so. I mean, I try to live a good life, you know? I do my best at work, I try to be a good neighbor... I don't have any dark secrets or terrible regrets keeping me up at night, I promise you that. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe sometimes I wish I could do a little more for the grandkids, but that's just normal grandfather stuff, right? Not real guilt. I certainly haven't done anything wrong that I'm dwelling on. I try to keep a clean conscience. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these right for you. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they feel like a chore. I still like doing the things I've always done. I mean, I still keep my yard looking nice, and I read the paper every morning. I'm not just sitting around the house all day staring at the wall, I promise. \n\nI guess... well, I haven't been out fishing in a few weeks, but that's just because the weather hasn't been great, and I've been a little tired from work. It's not that I don't enjoy it anymore. I just haven't gotten around to it. I still have all my hobbies and everything. \n\nIs that the kind of thing you meant? I just don't want you to worry that I'm giving up on things or losing interest in life, because I'm really not. I'm still keeping busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're going through the motions of your daily routine without any real motivation or purpose?\n\nPatient: Going through the motions? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I definitely still have a purpose. I mean, I go to work at the warehouse every day, and I make sure I do a good job filling the orders. They rely on me there, you know? I wouldn't want to let them down by just... phoning it in. \n\nI still have my routine, and I stick to it because it's important to stay productive. I get up, I make my coffee, I head to work... I'm not just wandering aimlessly, I promise you. I know it's just a stocking job, but it gives me a reason to get out of the house and keep moving. \n\nI guess maybe some days are a little harder to get started than others, especially since my wife passed, but I always push through. I'm not giving up on my responsibilities. \n\nDoes that answer your question alright? I just want to make sure you know I'm still trying my best every single day. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in activities that normally bring you pleasure over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Less interested? Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still enjoy my routine, you know? I still like getting my coffee in the morning and sitting on the porch. I haven't lost interest in the things that matter.\n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as eager to putter around in the garage lately, but that's just because I've been a bit tired after my shifts at the warehouse. It's not that I don't find pleasure in it anymore. I just need a little more rest these days, being 74 and all. I'm still the same old me, I promise.\n\nI hope I'm making sense. I just don't want you to think I'm sitting around feeling sorry for myself or losing my spark, because I'm really not. I'm doing alright, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not getting as much pleasure out of things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I still get plenty of enjoyment out of things. I mean, my morning coffee still tastes just as good, and I still like reading the paper. I'm not... I'm not finding everything dull or anything like that. \n\nI guess maybe... well, maybe things just feel a little quieter lately, you know? Since my wife's been gone, the house is just a bit still. But that doesn't mean I'm not enjoying my day-to-day life. I'm still finding things to smile about, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just miserable all the time, because I'm really trying to keep a good attitude. \n\nI'm doing the right things, aren't I? I just want to make sure I'm handling this all the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Oh, no, I don't think so. My clothes all seem to fit about the same as they always have. I haven't noticed any big changes or anything like that. \n\nI mean, I suppose I haven't really been weighing myself, but I'm still eating. I make sure I have my meals. Maybe my appetite isn't quite what it was when my wife was cooking, but I'm definitely not starving myself or wasting away. I know it's important to keep my strength up for the warehouse, so I make a point to eat. \n\nI'm taking good care of myself, I really am. That's what you're supposed to do, right? Just keep going and take care of your health?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer do?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I really don't. I mean, I know we just talked about this, but I'm still finding plenty of things to enjoy. I still like tending to the yard a bit on my days off, and I still watch the evening news. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I don't go to the diner for breakfast on Sundays like I used to. But that's just because it feels a little different sitting in that booth by myself, you know? It's not that I don't like the food or the people anymore. I just prefer to make my toast here at home now. It's perfectly fine. \n\nI'm still doing okay, aren't I? I mean, it's normal to change your routine a little bit at my age, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life changes or losses recently, such as the death of a loved one or a move to a new home?\n\nPatient: Well, besides losing my wife, which we talked about... I suppose there has been one other thing. I know it probably sounds silly to get worked up over a job at my age, but they recently changed my hours at the warehouse. And they brought in this whole new inventory system. I'm trying my absolute best to keep up with it, I really am. \n\nBut... well, to be perfectly honest, ever since they made those changes, I kind of feel like I'm falling apart a little bit. I hate to even admit it, because I don't want to sound like a complainer, but I've actually missed a couple of shifts. And I haven't been making it to my church commitments, which I feel just terrible about. I've even been snapping at my family, and they don't deserve that. Sometimes... sometimes in the mornings, I just sit there on the edge of the bed in my work boots, and I just wonder if I can even face going into the place anymore. \n\nIt's just a lot to learn all at once, you know? That's a normal way to react to a big schedule change, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or anything, because I really am trying to handle it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that normally bring you joy, but lately haven't been bringing you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I think that's sort of like what you asked just a minute ago, right? About the things I find pleasurable? But no, I really do still get happiness out of my usual things. \n\nLike I mentioned, I still like getting out in the yard, and I still love seeing my family, even if I admitted I was snapping at them a bit lately. I still get a lot of joy from them, I really do. I promise I'm not just sitting around the house moping all day. I try to keep a positive attitude and focus on the things I love, despite the trouble I'm having with the warehouse schedule. \n\nThat's the best way to handle things, don't you think? Just trying to focus on the good and stay upbeat? I really am trying my best to keep a good outlook.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to participate in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't really have to... force myself, no. When I have time for my yard work or seeing the family, I just do it because I want to. \n\nI mean, sure, I might be a little worn out from all the stress at the warehouse lately, but I don't have to drag myself to do the things I actually like. I'm still very willing to do my hobbies and keep busy. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, but I think wanting to do those things means I'm holding up alright, don't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to make an effort to find pleasure in things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I think it's just like I was saying before... I don't feel like I have to put in extra effort to enjoy things. \n\nWhen I'm out in the garden or with the grandkids, the joy is just there, naturally. I don't have to talk myself into being happy about it or anything like that. I just... I just enjoy it, plain and simple. \n\nI know you're asking a few different ways, and I hope I'm explaining it right. It's perfectly normal to still find joy in your hobbies even when work is stressful, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the answers you need to understand where I'm at.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist.
